## Document 024: 20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

## Document 115: 261025_gs_TW-PCB-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261025_gs_TW-PCB-CCL.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

PCB CCL upgrading continues: M10 is under qualification, which should start 
shipments from 2H27, while we expect M11 shipments to start no later than 2030  
For the PCB CCL industry overall, the key revenue/profit driver in our view is the solid 
specification upgrading trend (e.g., AI M7 ramp up in 2024, the M8 ramp in 2025, and 
M9 in 2026, Exhibit 3) which continues to drive high-end CCL players’ ASP growth by 
25-50% YoY annually in 2024-2026, and we are seeing the trend continuing and 
accelerating in coming years, despite the concerns of some investors in our 
conversations that a technology migration slowdown is impacting CCL players’ 
revenue/profit growth opportunity.  
During the TPCA show, we saw both EMC and TUC launch their M10/M11 solutions 
at the event, while both companies mentioned they are closely engaging with end 
customers for the next-generation AI R&D projects, based on the M10/11 solution (+ 
under qualification now). We believe EMC should start shipping its M10 products from 
2H27 at the earliest for specific AI customer’s GPU AI rack solutions and that shipments 
should ramp up significantly in 2028 as more GPU and ASIC AI projects start adopting 
this solution. For TUC’s M10 products, we believe the company will start shipment 
from 2028 for not only high-end 3.2T Ethernet switch projects but also for some ASIC 
projects.  
Both EMC and TUC are currently under verification with clients for their M10/M11 grade 
CCL, which incorporates Q-glass. In terms of the possibility of adopting PTFE-based 
CCL, the new solution continues to face production difficulties, making us less 
positive on the solution’s progress, and we only expect the MP (mass production) 
time to occur after 2H28 at the earliest (our supply chain checks suggest the 
production yield for PTFE-based PCB is running at 10-15% vs. average yield of 
80-90% for PCB with M9 grade CCL adopting Q-glass). As a result, we believe the PCB 
upgrade for switch tray in Nvidia’s Rubin Ultra GPU servers starting from 2H27 could 
have a high likelihood of adopting M9/M10 grade CCL with Q-glass/HC (Hydrocarbon) 
instead of PTFE, potentially implying better EMC/TUC share than we currently expect 
over the next 2-3 years).

to go down with customers’ investment in equipment or

New opportunity to enter the high ASP/GM ABF substrate supply chain for TW CCL 
supply chain players 
Given the relatively slow capacity investment to date from Japanese substrate material 
suppliers (ABF CCL/ T-glass/ micro thin copper foil etc.industries are mostly dominated 
by Japanese players, with more than 95% market share in the past 10+ years) while 
demand continues to trend up (we are seeing ABF substrates’ material usage doubling 
every 1-2 years, due to the expanding substrate size/ increasing substrate layer counts / 
decreasing substrate production yield rate), we started to see end customers 
qualification processes for Taiwan and mainland China ABF substrate supply chain 
vendors accelerate from 1H26, to better overcome supply constraints, implying a 
long term share gain trend from the Taiwan/mainland China new substrate material 
suppliers (including EMC and Co-Tech in our coverage space). We therefore believe 
EMC likely starts taking ABF substrate CCL market share from 2H26 (TAM could be 
up from US$0.8bn-1.2bn in 2026 to US$2.0bn-2.5bn in 2027 and US$4.0bn-4.5bn in

### Page 4

2028, accounting for ~15%/19%/22% of EMC’s 2026/27/28E revenue, per GSe, 
which not only suggests significant upside potential for EMC’s ASP outlook (ABF 
substrate CCL ASP per full sheet is ~US$1,500 vs. M7/8 only at US$130/180, Exhibit 
5) but should also lead to a solid GM uptrend. We estimate the ABF substrate CCL GM is 
now at 50%+ which could further increase closer to the industry average of ~70%+ 
in the long term based on our industry checks (vs. our 2026/27/28 GM estimate on 
EMC is 35.2%/40.7%/43.4%). Also, we believe that micro-thin copper foil, which is 
dominated by Japan vendors, should also start to see some new suppliers starting from 
late 2027/early 2028, while Co-Tech said that it is preparing R&D resources and is 
working on designs for the new production lines for this new substrate-level product, 
which we estimate could start contributing to the company’s revenue from 2H28 at the 
earliest per GSe.  
Moreover, considering that the ABF substrate size/layer count both continue to increase, 
with less favorable yield rate and production efficiency, we expect the high-end AI ABF 
substrates consumption on ABF CCL to increase significantly. Based on prevailing 
specifications, we believe a quarter sheet of ABF CCL can currently produce ~6-8 units 
of high-end ABF substrates, but that number could decrease significantly to 2-3 units 
per quarter sheet in 2028 based on the substrate design, which should lead to a solid 
unit volume demand uptrend.  
In terms of supply, EMC said that it could have 1.5mn sheets/month capacity by end of 
2027 for ABF substrate CCL during TPCA event, which would then likely account for 50% 
of the global ABF substrate CCL capacity on our estimates, suggesting a strong focus by 
the company to compete in this high ASP/GM product segment.

PCB/CCL upgrades to continue with limited risk from CPO adoption 
We see limited near term risk of Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) adoption as the industry 
should start with switch trays in GPU servers from 2028E, when we estimate switch 
board to account for 14% of a total CCL TAM of US$48bn in 2028E. In considering an 
extreme case where all switch boards are downgraded to M4/M7 grade CCL, we would 
expect the impact to result in a <10% reduction in the 2028E CCL TAM, an impact which 
we believe would be manageable. At the TPCA show, we saw ZDT showcase its 30 layers

HDI PCB switch board, and the company said that it is confident that switch board will

### Page 7

gainers
EMC to see solid upside on profit potential with the new substrate CCL opportunity
We continue to see EMC make progress in the new ABF substrate CCL business, a market
which the company entered in early 2026 and started producing high-end ABF substrate
CCL from 2H26, with monthly revenues reaching NT$150-200mn (<1% of the company’s
Sep monthly revenue) for the most high-end TPU substrate CCL market (see here). We
believe EMC could become one of the market leaders within the next 3-5 years (we are

expecting 20-30% market share in 2028E and trending up significantly afterward), given
its track record of successfully entering new markets over the past two decades.
Furthermore, the company is well-positioned to gain share in the tight supply market as
the company is proactive with its substrate CCL expansion plans, and the company
expects to expand its substrate CCL capacity to 4-5x of current level by 2027 (current
substrate CCL capacity at 300k sheet/month), which should account for 50% of the

global substrate CCL capacity by end of 2027E, implying a good opportunity for the
company to further ramp up market share in the long term.
M10/11 upgrades still the key to earnings growth for EMC & TUC in the next 3+
years, while the favorable S/D outlook should continue to drive the like-for-like
base pricing in coming quarters
We see EMC and TUC being well-positioned in the ongoing CCL upgrade trend as both
companies are currently under verification with clients for their respective next

generation M10/M11 grade CCL and are also future proofing their product-portfolios by
continuing to develop PTFE-based CCL. For the new M10 grade CCL, we expect EMC will
start shipments from 2Q27-3Q27 while TUC should start shipments from 2028, leading
to meaningful upside potential for each company’s revenue, as EMC/TUC’s current ASPs
have been approximately US$63/46 in 2026 while the M10 grade CCL ASP is currently
priced at ~US$800 per sheet, implying substantial ASP upside potential from here, all

else equal.
We continue to see a solid S/D outlook for high-end CCLs in coming years (see here) as
capacity expansion for these CCLs continues to track slower than demand growth (see
here). However, we note TUC revised up its capacity target and expects it to increase
2.5x+ over 2026-30 (8/5/30% upward revision to the year-end target capacity for
2026/27/28 from previous guidance), and based on company comments we believe the
new capacity is fully booked by clients, implying a solid revenue/profit growth outlook.

Co-Tech is currently obtaining LTAs to secure high-end copper foil capacity,
suggesting a solid long term market share and revenue outlook
We believe Co-Tech is well-positioned to benefit from the structural demand for high
end copper foil in a shortage environment, as the company announced capacity
expansion plans for a new Plant 4 in Malaysia, with capacity being secured by more than
one client, per the company. The company now target its annual HVLP3+ effective

capacity to reach 10K/20K/30K tons by end of 2027/28/29 (equivalent of
833/1666/2500 tons/month by 2027/28/29 end vs. current HVLP3+ effective capacity
of ~250 tons/month). This should enable the company to achieve further gains in market
share amid high end copper foil supply shortage environment (see here), as well as
seeing a better pricing environment going forward.
We expect Co-Tech’s new capacity, after ramp up, should continue to see 100% UTR, as

ZDT continues to maintain key one-stop shop status for all ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP 
products, suggesting solid market share outlook in long term 
We remain positive on ZDT as the company continues to make progress with its optical 
module mSAP and AI PCB business, and the company mentioned that both of these 
business are seeing capacity bookings by clients. We believe ZDT will become a market 
share leader in the optical module mSAP industry (see here), as we expect the company 
to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two years based on announced capex 
plans. Most suppliers in the optical module mSAP industry are proactively expanding 
capacity as CPO adoption risk remains limited.  
Also, considering that ZDT is the only company that manufactures all types of 
ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP products, it is well positioned for further share gains not only in 
the optical transceiver market, but also in the AI PCB, ABF, BT markets. Additionally, we 
estimate that these new products will likely carry 35-50%+ GMs in 2027, implying a solid 
revenue/profit outlook for the company going forward.  
We also see other companies within the CCL/IC substrate sector flagging supply 
tightness across the whole supply chain as industry expansion has not been sufficient to 
meet prevailing demand. This is in line with our view that suppliers with timely capacity 
expansion plans are better positioned to make further market share gains and benefit 
from stronger pricing/profitability amid a supply-constrained environment.

### Page 10

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$10,200 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers.

Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,010 is based on a 22x 2027E P/E (+2x 
STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).  
Key downside risks:  (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL segment; 
(2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch shipments 
globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Co-Tech 
Co-Tech is a global leading general server copper foil supplier, with ~60% market share 
in 2025 per GSe. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain opportunities in the 
near term, considering its proactive HVLP capacity expansion plan and strong 
know-how in the advanced copper foil industry. Also, adoption of HVLP4 copper foil 
from AI servers and 1.6T networking switches starting in 2H26 should be a key demand 
driver for Co-Tech, in our view. Additionally, the company’s proactive pricing strategy 
and ability to improve production yield should drive revenue momentum in the long 
term.  
We are Buy rated as we believe Co-Tech will see better revenue/profitability in the long 
term given its shift to a more premium product mix along with proactive capacity plans 
(Plant 3 to be ready by end of 2027) and this should lead to strengthened valuations.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$940 is based on a 22x 2028E P/E, which is 
+1SD above the company’s average upcycle valuation in the past 10-years. 
Key downside risks: (1) earlier than expected CPO adoption in the computing IC, (2) 
slower than expected yield rate improvement speed, and (3) much stronger than 
expected capacity growth from HVLP peers, limiting pricing power

### Page 12

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most

from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which

should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology,

including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle
average valuation).
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Idea
Yageo Corp.  | Asia Pacific
3Q preview – margin upside;
MLCC tightening thesis intact
3Q revenue exceeded guidance, and we expect OpM of 29.1%,
140bps above the Street. Robust AI and general server demand,
together with pricing, should offset consumer softness,
supporting 4Q revenue and margin guidance of “up slightly q/q.”
Stay OW; price target unchanged.
3Q preview –  better than guidance, driven by stronger volumes and pricing:
Unaudited revenue reached ~NT$49.5bn (+11% q/q, +50% y/y), stronger than

management's guidance of a "moderate increase q/q" (which we interpret as mid- to
high-single-digit growth). We did highlight in our results takeaway report (link) that
there was no pushback from management when we asked whether the guidance
was on the conservative side. We think  some conservatism was baked into the
guidance in view of uncertainties and limited visibility into the consumer segment.
As a result of the stronger top line, we now expect GM to surpass 40%, reaching

40.4% (+190bps q/q, +420bps y/y), and for OpM to increase to 29.1% (+270bps q/q,
+620bps y/y). This is still considered in line with management's  margin guidance of
up "slightly" q/q (which we interpret as 0-3% sequential growth). With the slightly
stronger top line and margin forecast, we now estimate 3Q net profit to come in at
~NT$11.6bn (+23% q/q, +82% y/y) for EPS of NT$5.63.
4Q outlook –  pricing and AI demand should continue to drive revenue growth

and margin expansion: Consumer demand is likely going to weaken in 4Q, based on
our supply chain checks, as well as PC demand coming in weaker than expected in
3Q, per IDC. However, AI and general server demand should remain robust, and
Yageo should continue to see strong momentum for non-consumer related passive
component demand.  As a result, we see management issuing 4Q revenue guidance
of "up slightly q/q" and GM/OpM guidance of "up slightly q/q".

Key issues on which we will focus in the earnings call: 1) demand visibility for non-
AI; 2) pricing actions for MLCC in 1Q, 3) pricing actions for tantalum, resistors,
magnetics, etc., 4) customer inventory levels for consumer, 5) thoughts on the TDK/
Taiyo Yuden MOU, 6) view on midrange/low-end capacity shortage in CY27.
Reiterate OW with price target at NT$1,050: This implies ~24x CY28e P/E;
however, PEG is only 0.72x. We favor Yageo for its scale, broad product portfolio

Morgan Stanley Research 7
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at NT$1,050:  Our price target is our base case
value, derived from our residual income valuation model. Similar to our analysis of other
tech hardware companies within our coverage, we think this methodology provides the
most accurate value of the firm by taking into account the cost of equity.
Our key assumptions are all unchanged, including:

• Cost of equity of 7.8%
° Risk-free rate of 1.0% (10-year Taiwanese government bond yield)
° Equity risk premium of 6.8%
° Beta of 1.1
• Medium-term growth rate of 12%
• Terminal growth rate of 3%
Our price target implies 32x 2027e P/E and 24x 2028e P/E. These are above its five-year
historical range of 8x to 18x, which we view as justified considering its longer-term
transformation story to become a global total solutions provider, moving from passive

into active components. In addition, compared to global passive component peers, we
believe Yageo stock remains undervalued with higher ROE and margins but lower P/E vs.
Japanese peers (which trade on an average at 30x+ P/E).
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$1,490 and NT$480,
respectively.

undersupply for the midrange/low end.

cycle is now back in play.
▪ Utilization rates are increasing, so
suppliers are expecting price hikes for the
midrange/low end. Distributors and ODMs
are both building inventory.
▪ AI demand is growing and tantalum
capacitor pricing may show further price
increases in 2H.
▪ All of this should help support a re-rating

in the stock.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 130: 261027_daiwa_TSMT

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TSMT.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: We met with Taiwan Surface Mounting Technology (TSMT) 
on 27 October. According to management, its product mix for 3Q26 was 
TFT-LCD 47%, Mini LED 9%, DRAM module 15%, auto 10%, cable 7%, 
touch 4% and others 8%. TSMT is positive on its optical transceiver, DRAM 
module, auto, low-earth orbit satellites (LEO), server and robotic segments. 
The QFII holding in TSMT was 29.7% on 22 October vs. 19.8% at the end 
of 2025.

Highlights: 4Q26 and 2027 outlook update – an upward revision. 
TSMT guided for its 4Q26 revenue to grow by 5-8% QoQ, and 2027 
revenue to grow by at least 20% (some upside potential subject to raw 
material conditions) for 2027 thanks to multiple revenue drivers, including 
optical transceivers, DRAM module, auto, LEO, server and robotic 
segments. For 4Q26 and 2027, the company expects its quarterly revenue 
and gross margin to further expand sequentially on a more favourable 
product mix from optical transceiver, LEO and server revenue. TSMT 
expects its gross margin to improve to 15-17% in 1H27 and over 20% in 
2H27 on the solid optical transceiver order outlook.

Strong revenue and margin outlook is expected to be driven mainly 
by the optical transceiver segment. TSMT mentioned several revenue 
drivers for 2027-28; among them, optical transceiver surface mounting 
technology (SMT) assembly is the most significant one. TSMT is a major 
global supplier for 800G and 1.6T products. A US-based client has noted 
that the 1.6T delivery schedule was ahead of its previous expectation (from 
4Q26 to 3Q26). The company has thus won an additional US-based tier-
one client (expected to have a larger contribution than the first optical client 
from 3Q27) and a China-based client over the past few months. TSMT is 
also in talks with a new US-based tier-one optical transceiver client. 
According to TSMT, optical transceiver revenue will likely account for 5-
10% of total revenue in 1Q27, 10-15% in 2Q27 and 15-20% in 3Q27; for 
2028, the company aims for 30-40%. Given the pure outsourcing (ie, 
includes no buying and selling of material) nature of the optical transceiver 
business, the segment brings upside potential to the gross margin level, 
per management. Aside from the optical transceiver segment, the company 
also sees strong revenue momentum in 2027-2028 from the DRAM module 
(2027 revenue up 30+% YoY), auto, robotic, LEO and server segments. 
TSMT says it has been expanding its global capacity and adjusting its 
equipment to fit different usages, eg, transforming from TFT-LCD to optical 
transceiver.

Valuation: The stock is currently trading at PERs of 24.8x/13.8x, based on 
Bloomberg’s 2026E/27E EPS forecasts.

27 October 2026

Optical segment to boost revenue and margins

➢ … driven by optical segment with sequentially increasing contribution 
➢ New optical transceiver client underway, on top of the existing 3

## Document 261: Business creations and bankruptcies in France_ double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, and stable non-performing loans (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business creations and bankruptcies in France_ double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, and stable non-performing loans (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

08/10/2026
1
October 2026
BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE
-
DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER GROWTH IN BANKRUPTCIES,
AND STABLE NON
-
PERFORMING LOANS
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLIC
ATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THE
RISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH
REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION ON A QUARTERLY BASIS.

### Page 2

Double
-
digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non
-
performing loans
2
ECOCHARTS
ECONOMIC STUDIES
•
Business
creations
rose
by
12
%
y/y
in
the
first
eight
months
of
2026
,
marking
an
acceleration
compared
with
the
growth
seen
in
2025
(+
5
.
3
%
)
.
•
This
increase
was
driven
by
sectors
associated
with
new
information
and
communication
technologies,
where
the
trend
is
gaining
momentum
.
•
Meanwhile,
business
bankruptcies

rose
by
5
.
3
%
y/y
from
January
to
July,
according
to
the
data
from
the
Banque
de
France
(vs
+
3
.
6
%
y/y
in
2025
)
.
After
a
more
pronounced
rise
in
the
first
quarter
(+
8
%
y/y),
this
increase
has
since
slowed
.
This
moderation
could
continue,
as,
according
to
Altares,
payment
delays
have
decreased
to
an
average
of
12
.
3
days
in
the
second
quarter
of
2026
(
14
.
1
days
a
year
earlier),
compared
with
the
European
average
of
13
.
1
days
.
•
The

rise
in
business
bankruptcies
is
most
pronounced
in
the
services
sector,
which
has
simultaneously
seen
an
increase
in
business
creations
.
This
trend
is
clear
in
the
retail
sector,
with
a
13
%
increase
in
business
creations
since
the
beginning
of
2026
,
while
business
bankruptcies
are
up
3
%
.
•
In
construction
and
manufacturing,
business
bankruptcies
have
been
declining
since
the
second
quarter
of
2026
.
•
The
level
of
the
average
ratio
of
non
-

performing
loans
is
moderately
high
and
has
remained
stable
for
the
past
year
(
4
.
0
%
of
total
loans
and
advances
in
Q
2
2026
)
.
More
cyclical
than
in
other
sectors
,
the
ratio
of
non
-
performing
loans
in
construction
reached
its
highest
level
since
2019
at
9
.
5
%
.
•
The
share
of
underperforming
loans,
but
not
yet
non
-
performing
(in
Stage
2
,
according
to
IFRS
9
terminology),
which
provides
an
indication
of
the
future
trend
in
non
-
performing

loans,
is
stable,
standing
at
15
.
4
%
q/q,
and
remains
below
its
all
-
time
high
(
16
.
1
%
)
.
•
Despite
a
significant
rise
since
2021
,
the
economic
weight
of
bankruptcies
–
which
allows
them
to
be
weighted
by
a
proxy
for
company
size
–
remains,
on
average,
below
its
Q
1
2025
peak
(
0
.
53
compared
with
0
.
61
,
respectively)
.
However,
the
economic
weight
of
bankrupted
small
businesses
stands
at
a
historically
high
level
(
2
.
11
)
.
For
more information, please refer to our publication:

C
hart of the
week
|
French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into perspective
.

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.
Exhibit 5: Higher Yields Meet Shifting Composition of U.S. Treasury Demand.
A) Higher 10-year Yield Consistent with Strong Nominal Growth Environment. B) Domestic Private Investors Now Dominate U.S. Treasury Market.

yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

## Document 278: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

### Page 2

ESG与可持续发展周报
3 方面考虑。一是能源革命是新一轮科技革命和产业变革的关键赛
道。当前，以绿色低碳为标志的能源革命正在全面跨过技术积累拐
点，进入到快速商业化规模化发展阶段。国际可再生能源署数据显
示，2010—2025 年，光伏、陆上风电度电成本分别累计下降 89%、
71%；2025 年全球超九成新建可再生能源发电项目成本低于化石能源
电站。国际能源署预计，2026 年全球能源总投资将达到 3.4 万亿美
元，其中 2.2 万亿美元投向风电、光伏、储能、氢能等清洁能源领
域，已成为全球布局新一轮科技革命和产业变革的重要方向。二是积
极推进能源革命是实现碳达峰碳中和的必由之路。从排放源头看，能
源活动贡献了我国 80%以上的碳排放量，其中电力行业碳排放超过全
社会碳排放的 40%，实现碳达峰碳中和目标的主要矛盾集中在以化石
能源为主的能源结构上。中国式现代化以实体经济为根基，能源的饭
碗必须端在自己手里。中长期看，我国能源刚性需求仍将继续增长。
从源头上减碳是实现碳达峰碳中和的治本之策，要求我们必须通过清

洁能源规模化扩张，覆盖新增用能需求，减少化石能源消费存量。三
是发展新质生产力需要通过能源革命来提供可靠安全保障。当前，地
区冲突频发加剧地缘政治风险，能源问题政治化、武器化属性更加突
出，全球能源市场波动加剧。发展新质生产力，建设现代化产业体
系，特别是满足全国一体化算力网、新一代通信网等新型基础设施建
设，以及集成电路、人工智能、新能源汽车等新兴产业用能增长需
求，推动产业高端化、智能化、绿色化，要求我们积极推动能源革
命，持续提高国内能源供给占比。
《意见》围绕着力推进发展方式创新，部署构建绿色低碳循环发
展的经济体系、优化绿色发展政策工具箱和积极推进能源革命三项举
措。我们认为，新质生产力推动可持续发展，我国正迈入以新质生产
力为核心驱动的绿色发展新阶段。
 行业动态
（1）近年来，我国保险业在面对重大自然灾害时，响应速度与理赔力
度不断提升，社会保障功能持续显现。今年 5月以来，受强降雨、台
风等影响，广西、湖北、甘肃等地接连发生城市内涝、水库溃坝、山
体滑坡等灾情。中国保险行业协会数据显示，截至 2026年 7月末，面

对 20 个省份的暴雨、台风等灾害，保险业已快速赔付 55.3 亿元。
2025 年全年，保险业针对重大自然灾害的赔付高达约 346.72 亿元。
当前我国保险赔付占自然灾害直接经济损失的比重仅约 10%，仍有较
大保障缺口。巨灾往往具有低频高损、破坏力极强的特点，仅靠事后
被动补偿，社会整体损失依然沉重。因此，破局的关键在于前置——
推动保险服务从出险以后赔向灾害来临前防延伸。前置风险防线，重
点在科技预警和风险减量。保险机构应把服务链条前移，建立覆盖多
灾种的监测预警和应急响应机制，运用气象监测、遥感、地理信息、
物联网等技术，对重点区域、重点标的进行动态跟踪，在灾害来临前
向客户精准预警，协助做好人员转移、设施加固、农作物抢收等防护
工作。将防灾减损费用纳入产品设计，与应急管理、气象、水利等部
门建立信息共享和联动机制，推动灾前预防、灾中干预、灾后理赔有
效衔接，真正降低灾害损失。
（2）10月 14日，全国首批绿证跨境集中交易（香港）发布活动在广
州举行，本次交易合计成交约 10万个国家可再生能源绿色电力证书，

对应可再生能源电量近 1亿千瓦时。本次交易实现内地绿证规模化进
入香港市场，搭建起内地绿证市场对接香港国际绿色金融市场的流通
渠道，是粤港澳大湾区推进绿色能源协同创新的标志性实践。

### Page 3

ESG与可持续发展周报
 技术前沿
10 月 15 日，中国石油塔里木 120 万吨乙烯工程商业运营新闻发
布会在北京举行，项目即日起全面投入商业运营。标志着中国石油在
新疆形成 300万吨级乙烯产业长周期稳定运营能力。作为我国首个全
链条绿色低碳乙烯工程、中西部单体规模最大乙烯项目，工程总投资
256.56 亿元，整体国产化率 99%。可年产 90 万吨全密度聚乙烯、45
万吨聚丙烯、10万吨顺丁橡胶，橡塑产品总产能达 145万吨，填补南
疆高端聚烯烃、合成橡胶新材料产业空白，带动区域下游形成千亿级
产业集群。
自今年 7月 16 日一次开车成功以来，工程已累计生产乙烯超 26
万吨，外供优质化工品 56.6 万吨，各类产品合格率达 100%，聚丙
烯、MTBE（甲基叔丁基醚）产品远销中亚市场。除此之外，工程搭
建“绿电直连+碳捕集+蓝氢+蓝氨+绿色化肥”全链条低碳技术闭环，
建成国内首套自主技术乙烯三机电驱装置和全球首个乙烯裂解炉烟气
碳循环利用示范基地。通过回收乙烯裂解炉烟气中的二氧化碳生产尿

素，实现“以废制肥”。目前，工程已捕集利用二氧化碳超 10万吨，
产出绿色尿素超 12万吨。工程可年减碳 137万吨，固碳生态效益相当
于新增 548万亩森林碳汇。
 研究探索
近日，由中国经济信息社主办的“蓄势与新篇：2026耐心资本与
可持续发展大会”在湖南长沙举行。在大会上，中国金融学会绿色金
融专业委员会主任、北京绿色金融与可持续发展研究院院长马骏表
示，我国逐步形成了以界定标准、信息披露、激励机制、产品体系为
四大支柱的绿色金融体系，绿色金融规模持续扩大。马骏指出，钢
铁、水泥、有色、化工等高碳行业低碳转型的融资需求，可能超过光
伏、风电等纯绿活动。应借助绿色科技提升转型项目的经济效益，识
别兼具减碳效益和经济性的技术路径。由其牵头筹建、获香港特区政
府支持的“绿色加速器”公益平台已经启动，将支持我国绿色科技企
业在“一带一路”沿线落地可融资项目，助力绿色企业出海。中国能
源研究会能源金融与法律分会主任委员陈景东在主旨演讲“绿色金
融——政策与实践”中表示，绿色财税和绿色金融为能源绿色低碳转

型发挥了重要作用。随着新能源行业不断发展，财税政策正在逐步调
整完善，绿色金融的支持政策和实践将更多来自资本市场。在绿色转
型过程中，能源企业需要更多权益类资金，希望财税和金融部门继续
加大对能源行业转型的支持。
 国际合作
10 月 20-22 日，“2026 可持续全球领导者大会”将在黄浦滨江
拉开帷幕。大会将汇聚来自全球的 300余位顶级嘉宾，包括联合国相
关机构、多个国际组织的代表、诺贝尔奖与图灵奖得主、国内外领军
企业高管及知名智库专家，围绕全球可持续发展的现实挑战与机遇开
展高水平对话。本届大会共有 22 个分论坛，议题涵盖：AI 发展与人
性未来，气候治理与生态保护，清洁能源与安全保障，绿色消费与品
牌使命，教育转型与科技赋能等。
 投资建议
《关于发展新质生产力的意见》围绕着力推进发展方式创新，部
署构建绿色低碳循环发展的经济体系、优化绿色发展政策工具箱和积
极推进能源革命三项举措。我们认为，新质生产力推动可持续发展，
